play no role in the construction of the Fourier-Galerkin approximation. The same approach could be applied to
more general costs from
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, replacing the convexity properties of

{\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}
by a suitable monotonicity properties.
The convergence results exposed in Section
4
can be
adapted in a direct way.

Our choice to focus on mean field control mostly comes from the fact that the Fourier-Galerkin approximation
has a clear interpretation since it can be connected itself with a finite-dimensional control problem (see the two previous paragraphs).
When dealing with a general mean field game, the interpretation of the
system (

⁢
F

B
N

) as a mean field game system is certainly less obvious because one should then regard
(
2.4
)
as the flow of marginal laws of one player. Although conceivable, this would be rather artificial.
Apart from this, the question is meaningful and we indeed plan to come back to the application to mean field games,
but from a numerical prospect, in a future work.

4.
Convergence results

Now that we have shown the well-posedness of the approximating problem, we can address its convergence to the mean field control problem (
2.3
) presented in Subsection
2.1.1
. As discussed in Subsection
2.2.1
, we recall that under Assumptions
2.2
and
2.10
, the forward-backward system (

⁢
F

B
∞

), that we re-state here

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mu^{\infty}_{t}&=\Delta\mu^{\infty}_{t}+\mathrm{div}(\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})\mu^{\infty}_{t}),\quad t\in[0,T],\\
\mu^{\infty}_{0}&=m,\\
\partial_{t}u_{t}^{\infty}&=-\Delta u_{t}^{\infty}+H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot),\quad t\in[0,T],\\
u^{\infty}_{T}&=\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot),\end{cases}

allows us to characterize the optimal trajectory
\mu^{\infty}
together with the optimal control
\alpha^{*,\infty}(\cdot)=-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}(\cdot))
. Similarly, in Section
3
we showed that under Assumptions
2.2
and
2.10
, the system (

⁢
F

B
N

), that is

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mu^{N}_{t}&=\Delta\mu^{N}_{t}+\mathrm{div}(\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N})),\quad t\in[0,T],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\mu^{N}_{0}&=m,\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\partial_{t}u^{N}_{t}&=-\Delta u^{N}_{t}+H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0ptu^{N}_{T}&=\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N},\end{cases}

gives us the optimal pair
\mu^{N}
and
\alpha^{*,N}(\cdot)=-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}(\cdot))
for the approximating MFC problem (
2.5
), whenever
N
is chosen large enough.

We establish three different results of convergence, each with explicit rates. First, we address the convergence of the approximating optimal control in the
L^{2}
sense. This is done
in Subsection
4.1

by means of a stability argument based on the monotonicity property of the coefficients.
Then, the convergence is shown to hold with respect to the uniform norm under an additional condition on the terminal cost
\mathcal{G}
, see Subsection
4.2
. In the same subsection, similar results are obtained for the optimal trajectory. Finally, in Subsection
4.3
, we study the convergence of the value function
V^{N}
defined by (
2.5
) to the value function of the original MFC problem. We identify subsets of
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
on which
the convergence is uniform.

In most of the results exposed in this section,
the approximating forward-backward system (

⁢
F

B
N

) (and thus the approximating MFC control problem) is initiliazed from the same
initial condition
m

as the original system
(

⁢
F

B
∞

). This makes the presentation easier.
However, in line with Remark

3.23
and with our initial objective, we show in Proposition 4.4 that
the main result remains true if one replaces
m
by
m*D^{N}

in the initial condition of
(

⁢
F

B
N

).

To conclude, we emphasize that for the remainder of this section, we will consider again
N\geq\widetilde{N}
according to Remarks
3.10
and
3.19
.

4.1.
L^{2}
convergence of the approximating optimal control

Our aim here is to establish the convergence of
\alpha^{*,N}
to
\alpha^{*,\infty}
by exploiting the monotonicity of
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}
and
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
(that is, the convexity of
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, see Remark
2.4
). In particular, we will once again take inspiration from the usual Lasry-Lions monotonicity argument (which we already alluded to in the proof of Proposition

3.21
, see also e.g.
[
8
, Lemma 3.1.2]
). This result, see Theorem
4.2
below, gives us the rate of convergence of the approximating optimal control in a suitable
L^{2}
norm. Its proof relies on the following auxiliary estimate for
\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}
:

Lemma 4.1
.

Let Assumptions
2.2
and
2.10
hold. Then there exists a positive constant
C=C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)
such that

(4.1)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right).

Proof.

We have that
(\mu^{\infty}-\mu^{N})
solves

(4.2)

\partial_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t})=\Delta(\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t})+\mathrm{div}(\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N})).

By integrating the equation against
(\mu^{\infty}-\mu^{N})
and then by Young’s inequality it follows

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}

\displaystyle-\mu^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\mu^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla\mu^{N}_{s}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})\mu^{\infty}_{s}-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{s})(\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N})\rVert_{2}\lVert\nabla\mu^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla\mu^{N}_{s}\rVert_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})\mu^{\infty}_{s}-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})(\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N})\rVert^{2}_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla\mu^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla\mu^{N}_{s}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Thus,

\displaystyle\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\Big\lVert\Big(\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{s})\Big)\mu^{\infty}_{s}\Big\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}\Big\lVert\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{s})(\mu^{\infty}_{s}-{\mu^{{\infty}}_{s}*D^{N}})\Big\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{0}^{t}\Big\lVert\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{s})(\mu^{{\infty}}_{s}-\mu^{N}_{s})*D^{N}\Big\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\left(\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla u^{N}_{s}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\int_{0}^{t}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{s}-\mu^{N}_{s}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right),

where the last inequality follows from the
L^{\infty}
bound (
2.7
) on
\mu^{\infty}
(for the first term), the boundedness of
\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}(\cdot))
(for the second and third terms) and the fact that
\mu^{{\infty}}_{s}\in H^{q-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
(for the second term, in combination with Lemma
A.5
). Moreover we used the Lipschitz property of the mapping
p\mapsto\mathrm{D}_{p}H(x,p)
uniformly with respect to
x
, which is entailed by Assumption
2.2
- (H.5). Finally, we obtain (
4.1
) from Gronwall’s inequality.
∎

We can now state and prove the main result of this subsection.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let Assumptions
2.2
and
2.10
hold. Then there exists a positive constant
C=C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
such that

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{2(q-1)}}\lVert m\rVert^{4}_{2,q-1}.

Proof.

First, we notice that
(u^{\infty}-u^{N})
solves in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}

(4.3)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}(u^{\infty}_{t}-u^{N}_{t})&=-\Delta(u^{\infty}_{t}-u^{N}_{t})+(H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})*D^{N})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}),\\
\quad\\
(u^{\infty}_{T}-u^{N}_{T})(x)&=\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},x)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}(x),\end{cases}

whilst
(\mu^{\infty}-\mu^{N})
solves (
4.2
).
We will follow the duality argument often used in the literature on mean field games (see, e.g.
[
8
, Lemma 3.1.2]
)): integration by parts leads to

(4.4)

\displaystyle\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}

\displaystyle-\mu^{N}_{t},u^{\infty}_{t}-u^{N}_{t}\rangle

(4.5)

\displaystyle=-\langle\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N}),\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})*D^{N}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}\rangle

(4.6)

\displaystyle=-\langle\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N})-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N}),\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rangle

(4.7)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})*D^{N}-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})*D^{N}\rangle

(4.8)

\displaystyle\quad-\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}\rangle

(4.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}-\mu^{\infty}_{t}),\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rangle

(4.10)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})*D^{N}\rangle.

Regarding (
4.6
) and (
4.7
), from the strong convexity of
H
in the
p
argument
and the symmetry of
D^{N}
, we have, for a constant
C>0
,

\eqref{eqn: dualprod_1}+\eqref{eqn: dualprod_2}\leq-C\langle(\mu^{N}_{t}+\mu^{\infty}_{t})*D^{N},\lvert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rvert^{2}\rangle,

and thanks to the symmetry of
D^{N}
again, we can write (
4.8
) as

(
4.8
)

\displaystyle=-\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}-\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\langle(\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t})*D^{N},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)\rangle.

Notice that
\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}-\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)\rangle\geq 0
due to the monotonicity of
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}
and the fact that
\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}
and
\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N}
are both probability measures.
Therefore, by integrating in time (
4.4
) we arrive at the following expression

\displaystyle\langle\mu^{\infty}_{T}

\displaystyle-\mu^{N}_{T},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}\rangle-\langle\mu^{\infty}_{0}-\mu^{N}_{0},u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{N}_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}C\langle(\mu^{N}_{t}+\mu^{\infty}_{t})*D^{N},\lvert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rvert^{2}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\langle(\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t})*D^{N},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}-\mu^{\infty}_{t}),\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})*D^{N}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t.

For the term in the left hand side containing
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
, we can proceed as for
(
4.8
)
. Thus, by re-arranging the terms, we obtain

(4.11)

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{T}\langle(\mu^{N}_{t}+\mu^{\infty}_{t})*

\displaystyle D^{N},\lvert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rvert^{2}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\langle\mu^{\infty}_{T}*D^{N}-\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)\rangle

(4.13)

\displaystyle+\langle(\mu^{\infty}_{T}-\mu^{N}_{T})*D^{N},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)\rangle

(4.14)

\displaystyle+\langle\mu^{\infty}_{T}-\mu^{N}_{T},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)\rangle

(4.15)

\displaystyle+\langle\mu^{\infty}_{0}-\mu^{N}_{0},u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{N}_{0}\rangle

(4.16)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\langle(\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t})*D^{N},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.17)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{t},\cdot)\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.18)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})(\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}-\mu^{\infty}_{t}),\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.19)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t},H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})*D^{N}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t.

First, we notice from the monotonicity of
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
that
\eqref{eqn: monestimate_1}\leq 0
, and, since
\mu^{\infty}
and
\mu^{N}
have the same initial condition
m
, it also holds that
\eqref{eqn: monestimate_4}=0
(in Proposition
4.4
we discuss the case
\mu^{N}_{0}=m*D^{N}
). Now, to deal with the remaining terms, we want to exploit the spatial regularity of
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
and
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}
, as well as the smoothness of
\mu^{\infty}
and
u^{\infty}
.
To handle (
4.13
), we first rely on the Lipschitz property
of
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}

with respect to the
L^{2}
norm:

\eqref{eqn: monestimate_2}\leq\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{T}-\mu^{N}_{T}\rVert_{2}\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)\rVert_{2}\leq C(d,\mathcal{G})\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{T}-\mu^{N}_{T}\rVert_{2}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{T}*D^{N}-\mu^{\infty}_{T}\rVert_{2}.

Then, Young’s inequality, Lemma
4.1
and the regularity of
\mu^{\infty}
given by Proposition
2.12
combined with Lemma
A.5
entail that, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

(
4.13
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{T}-\mu^{N}_{T}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{T}*D^{N}-\mu^{\infty}_{T}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{\varepsilon^{-1}}{N^{2(q-1)}}\right).

Similarly, by the Lipschitz property of
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}
, we obtain the same estimate (possibly with a different constant) for (
4.16
).

Regarding (
4.14
), since
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu,\cdot)\in\mathrm{C}^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
for every
\mu
, with
\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu,\cdot)\rVert_{\infty,q}
independent of
\mu
, we have for any
\varepsilon>0

(
4.14
)

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{T}-\mu^{N}_{T}\rVert_{2}\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{T}-\mu^{N}_{T}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{-1}}{2}\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{N^{2(q-1)}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}+\varepsilon\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{\varepsilon^{-1}}{N^{2q}}\right),

where we used Lemma
A.5
to control
\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)\rVert_{2}
, recalling that
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu,\cdot)\in\mathrm{C}^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\subset H^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
. In the same manner and by noticing that the estimates on the
L^{2}
norm of
\mu^{\infty}
and
\mu^{N}
are uniform in time, we obtain an analogue bound for (
4.17
).

For the last terms (
4.18
) and (
4.19
), the convergence is guaranteed by the fact that
\mu^{\infty}_{t}\in H^{q-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
\nabla u_{t}^{\infty}\in\mathrm{C}^{{q-1}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, with norms bounded by constants independent of
t
. In particular, from Propositions
2.11
and
2.12
, Remark
3.12
and Lemma
A.5
, we have for any
\varepsilon>0

(
4.18
)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})\rVert_{\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}\rVert_{2}\lVert\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\frac{\varepsilon^{-1}}{\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\frac{\varepsilon^{-1}}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\varepsilon\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Moreover, by leveraging again Lemma
4.1
, Remark
3.12
and Lemma
A.5
, we can argue as we did for (
4.14
) and obtain that, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

(
4.19
)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}\lVert H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})*D^{N}\rVert_{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{N^{2(q-1)}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}+\varepsilon\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{\varepsilon^{-1}}{N^{2({q-1})}}\right).

Regarding the left-hand side, we have

\eqref{eqn: monestimate_lhs}\geq\int_{0}^{T}\langle\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N},\lvert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rvert^{2}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t\geq\bar{\gamma}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

Here we used the fact that
\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N}
is a positive measure, together with
\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}\geq\bar{\gamma}>0
for
N
large enough (see Lemma
2.13
).

Finally, by suitably tuning the various
\varepsilon
(we choose them
\sim\lVert m\rVert^{-2}_{2,q-1}
) and by combining all the previous estimates, we obtain

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{2(q-1)}}\lVert m\rVert^{4}_{2,q-1}.

Notice that the presence of the sole term
\lVert m\rVert^{4}_{2,q-1}
in the right-hand side is due to the fact that
\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}\geq\lVert m\rVert_{1}=1
, and so
\max\left\{\lVert m\rVert^{4}_{2,q-1},\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1},1\right\}=\lVert m\rVert^{4}_{2,q-1}
.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

The fourth power of
\lVert m\rVert_{2,{q-1}}

in the statement of Theorem

4.2

may seem rather unusual. In fact, it arises solely as a consequence of the estimates for
the two terms (
4.13
) and (
4.16
). Specifically,
computations for these two quantities lead to upper bounds comprising products of the form

\varepsilon^{-1}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}
, whereas
all the other terms of order
\varepsilon^{-1}
handled in the proof
do not include the extra factor
\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}
.
When choosing
\varepsilon\sim\lVert m\rVert^{-2}_{2,q-1}
, we obtain the term
\lVert m\rVert^{4}_{2,q-1}
.

Of course, one may wonder about the optimality of the exponent. In this regard, the first observation is that
the additional factor
\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}
appearing in the estimates for (
4.13
) and (
4.16
) comes from the available bounds for
(\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}-\mu^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2})_{0\leq t\leq T}
.
Basically, the latter can be controlled by
Lemma

A.5
, with sharp bounds depending on the smoothness of
(\mu_{t}^{\infty})_{0\leq t\leq T}
.
Up to this point, we do not see any way to modify the proof. The second
observation is that, in our proof, we have just controlled
(\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2,q-1})_{0\leq t\leq T}

by
\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}
. This is the most direct and systematic way to proceed (and leads to the final bound in Theorem

4.2
). Our feeling
is that this could be potentially improved by using further smoothing properties
of the Fokker-Planck equation in
(

⁢
F

B
∞

): in positive time
t\in(0,T]
,

\mu^{\infty}_{t}
has better regularity than

m
and lives in a higher Sobolev space.
Combining the higher regularity with some interpolation inequality and the fact that
\lVert\mu_{t}^{\infty}\rVert_{1}=1

for any
t\in[0,T]
, we think it is possible to prove that, for large values of
q
(with respect to
d
) and for
\alpha\in(0,1)
small,
there exist two constants
C
and
\theta\in(0,1)
such that

\lVert\mu_{t}^{\infty}\rVert_{2,q-1}\leq\frac{C}{t^{\alpha}}\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}^{\theta}.

Inserting the above bound in Lemma

A.5
, this could be a way to (slightly) decrease the exponent 4 appearing in
Theorem

4.2
.

We emphasize that Theorem

4.2

still applies when (

⁢
F

B
N

) starts from the truncated measure
\mu^{N}_{0}=m*D^{N}
instead of
m
:

Proposition 4.4
.

Let Assumptions
2.2
and
2.10
hold.
Initialize (

⁢
F

B
N

) from

\mu^{N}_{0}=m*D^{N}
. Then,
the conclusion of
Theorem

4.2

remains true.

Proof.

We just provide a sketch of proof.
In comparison with the proof of Theorem
4.2
, the only difference comes from the term (
4.15
), which is not identically equal to
0
.
So, the only difficulty is to show that, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

(4.20)

\displaystyle\lvert\langle

\displaystyle\mu^{\infty}_{0}-\mu^{N}_{0},u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{N}_{0}\rangle\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon^{-1}\frac{\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}}{N^{2(q-1)}}\right).

Once (
4.20
) has been established, one can tune tune
\varepsilon
as done in the proof of Theorem
4.2
in order to reach the same conclusion.

In the rest of the proof, we just address the derivation of (
4.20
).
First, using the fact that
m\in H^{q-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we deduce from Young’s inequality and Lemma
A.5
that, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

(4.21)

\lvert\langle\mu^{\infty}_{0}-\mu^{N}_{0},u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{N}_{0}\rangle\rvert\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\lVert m-m*D^{{N}}\rVert^{2}_{2}+\varepsilon\lVert u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{N}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\frac{\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}^{2}}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\varepsilon\lVert u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{N}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}.

Let us focus on
\lVert u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{N}_{0}\rVert^{2}_{2}
. As an initial step, let us observe that Lemma
4.1
holds even if
\mu^{N}_{0}=m*D^{N}
. The proof is almost the same. The only change comes from the difference between the initial conditions, but it can be estimated by means of Lemma
A.5
. Then,
forming the difference between the two equations
solved by
u^{\infty}
and
u^{N}
respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lVert u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{N}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla u^{N}_{s}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.22)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}\rVert_{2}^{2}

(4.23)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\left\lvert\langle\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{s},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N},\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla u^{N}_{s}\rangle\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\left\lvert\langle H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{s})*D^{N},\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla u^{N}_{s}\rangle\right\rvert\,\mathrm{d}s.

Regarding (
4.22
), the regularity of
\mathcal{G}
and
\mu^{N}
combined with Lemmas
A.3
and
A.5
and the new version of Lemma
4.1
entails

(
4.22
)

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\lVert\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T},\cdot)*D^{N}-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}^{2}\left(\frac{\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}^{-2}}{N^{2q}}+\frac{1}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right).

For (
4.23
),we can proceed similarly, up to an additional
use of Young’s inequality to separate

\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla u^{N}_{s}
from the difference of the two
\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{F}}
terms.

The last term can be treated by using Young’s inequality and then by leveraging the smoothness of
H
:

(
4.24
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})*D^{N}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})*D^{N}-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{s})*D^{N}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\left(\frac{1}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right).

By combining the bounds for
(
4.22
), (
4.23
)
and (
4.24
), and by recalling that
\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}\geq 1
, we get

\lVert u^{\infty}_{0}-u^{{N}}_{0}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2(q-1)}}+\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right).

We can finally plug this estimate in (
4.21
) and obtain (
4.20
).
∎

Theorem
4.2

and Proposition

4.4

imply a similar result for the convergence of the optimal control.

Corollary 4.5
.

Let Assumptions
2.2
and
2.10
hold.
Whether (

⁢
F

B
N

) be initialized from

\mu^{N}_{0}=m

or

\mu^{N}_{0}=m*D^{N}
, there exists a positive constant
C=C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
such that

\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{2(q-1)}}\lVert m\rVert^{4}_{2,q-1}.

Proof.

It is enough to notice that, for any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
, the mapping
p\mapsto\mathrm{D}_{p}H(x,p)
is Lipschitz uniformly with respect to
x
. Indeed this is a consequence of Assumption
2.2
- (H.5). Thus, the result follows from Theorem
4.2

or
Proposition
4.4
.
∎

4.2.
Convergence in
L^{\infty}
norm of the optimal trajectory and control

From Theorem
4.2
, we can deduce estimates for the convergence of
\mu^{N}
to
\mu^{\infty}
in both
L^{2}
and
L^{\infty}
norms. Consequently, we can also analyze the convergence of the optimal feedback in
L^{\infty}
norm (and not only in
L^{2}
as done in Corollary
4.5
). To derive the estimates with respect to the
L^{\infty}
norm, we follow the approach initially used in Proposition
3.11
, and based on Duhamel’s formula.

To address the uniform convergence of the control, we need more regularity on
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
.

Assumption 4.6
.

Let
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}\colon\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be as in Assumption
2.2
. We require that, for any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
, the mapping
\mu\mapsto\nabla\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu,x)
is Lipschitz with respect to
L^{2}
, with a constant independent of
x
.

We emphasize that we require this additional assumption only for
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
and not for
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}
. The rationale behind this distinction will become evident in the proof of Theorem
4.8
.

Remark 4.7
.

We can notice that if
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{C}^{2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
, with
(y,z)\mapsto\delta_{\mu}^{2}\mathcal{G}(\mu,y,z)
being twice continuously differentiable and
z\mapsto\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}^{2}\mathcal{G}(\mu,y,z)

being bounded in
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
uniformly in
(y,\mu)
(which is the case if the mapping is jointly continuous in the three variables
(\mu,y,z)
when

\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is equipped with
W_{1}
), then
\nabla\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\cdot,y)
is Lipschitz in the measure argument with respect to
L^{2}
, with a constant uniform in space. Indeed, by definition of the linear derivative, it holds for any
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,

\lvert\nabla\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu,y)-\nabla\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\prime},y)\rvert\leq\lVert\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}^{2}\mathcal{G}(\mu,y,\cdot)\rVert_{2}\lVert\mu-\mu^{\prime}\rVert_{2}.

Theorem 4.8
.

Let Assumptions
2.2
and
2.10
hold. Then there exists a positive constant
C=C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})
such that

(4.25)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1},

Moreover, if Assumption
4.6
is in force, it also holds

(4.26)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1-\frac{d}{2}}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1},

and

(4.27)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{\infty}+\lVert\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})\rVert_{\infty})\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1-\frac{d}{2}}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}.

Proof.

We first notice that
(
4.25
) easily follows by plugging the result of Theorem
4.2
in the estimate of Lemma
4.1
.

We now turn to (
4.27
). The proof is very similar to the one of Proposition
3.11
, and we will sketch here the main lines. By Duhamel’s representation formula we have, for any
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}(x)-\nabla

\displaystyle u^{N}_{t}(x)

(4.28)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}p_{T-t}(x-y)\Big[\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}](\mu^{\infty}_{T},y)-\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}](\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}(y)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}y

(4.29)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\nabla p_{s-t}(x-y)\left[H(y,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}(y))-{H}(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{s})*D^{N}(y)\right]\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.30)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\nabla p_{s-t}(x-y)\Big[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{s},y)-\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}(y)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s.

A first term to control is

\displaystyle\lvert\eqref{eqn: est_inf_grad_2}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\lvert\nabla p_{s-t}(x-y)\rvert\big\lvert H(y,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}(y))-{H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})*D^{N}(y)}\big\rvert\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\lvert\nabla p_{s-t}(x-y)\rvert\big\lvert\bigl(H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s})-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{s})\bigr)*D^{N}(y)\big\rvert\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)}{N^{q-1-\frac{d}{2}}}+C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\int_{t}^{T}\frac{N^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\sqrt{s-t}}\lVert\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla u^{N}_{s}\rVert_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

where we used Lemma

A.6
to get the first line together with Lemma
A.3
to get the second line.

Admit now for a while that (the proof is given a few lines below)

(4.31)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}-\nabla u^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1}},

and then deduce that

\displaystyle\lvert\eqref{eqn: est_inf_grad_2}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1-\frac{d}{2}}}.

Regarding the term involving
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
, we have
(recalling the notation
p_{t}(x)
for the heat kernel at
(t,x)
)

\displaystyle\lvert\eqref{eqn: est_inf_grad_1}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}p_{T-t}(x-y)\big\lvert\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}](\mu^{\infty}_{T},y)-\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}]({\mu^{\infty}_{T}*D^{N}},y)\big\rvert\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}p_{T-t}(x-y)\big\lvert\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}]({\mu^{\infty}_{T}*D^{N}},y)-\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}](\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},y)\big\rvert\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}p_{T-t}(x-y)\big\lvert\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}](\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},y)-\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}](\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N},\cdot)*D^{N}(y)\big\rvert\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})\left(\frac{\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}}{N^{q-1}}+\frac{\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}}{N^{q-1}}+\frac{1}{N^{{q-1-\frac{d}{2}}}}\right),

where we used the fact that
\nabla[\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}](\cdot,y)
is Lipschitz (with respect to
L^{2}
, uniformly in
y
) together with
Proposition

2.12
and Lemma
A.5
to control the first term
and then invoked
(
4.25
) to control the second term.
The third term is handled by means of
Lemma
A.5
again
together with
the smoothness in space of
\nabla\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
.
In a similar manner, we obtain

\lvert\eqref{eqn: est_inf_grad_3}\rvert\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})\left(\frac{\lVert m\rVert_{2,q-1}}{N^{q-1}}+\frac{\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}}{N^{q-1}}+\frac{1}{N^{q-\frac{d}{2}}}\right).

By the Duhamel representation formula, we obtain the desired estimate for
\lVert\nabla u^{N}_{t}-\nabla u^{\infty}_{t}\rVert_{\infty}
. To conclude, the estimate on
\lVert\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}_{t})-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}_{t})\rVert_{\infty}
follows again from the Lipschitz property of
\mathrm{D}_{p}H
.

In order to complete the proof of
(
4.27
), one must now establish
(
4.31
). In fact, using
(
4.25
) as we have just done a few lines above (combined with the
L^{2}
-Lipschitz continuity of
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}
and
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
in the measure argument), the bound can be obtained by following the proof of Lemma
3.6
.

It now remains to prove (
4.26
). It follows from the same argument as the one used in the proof of Proposition
3.18
combined with Theorem
4.2
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\mu^{\infty}_{t}

\displaystyle(x)-\mu^{N}_{t}(x)

(4.32)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\nabla p_{t-s}(x-y)\Big[\mathrm{D}_{p}H(y,\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}(y))-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(y,\nabla u^{N}_{s}(y))\Big]\mu^{\infty}_{s}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.33)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\nabla p_{t-s}(x-y)\mathrm{D}_{p}H(y,\nabla u^{N}_{s}(y))\Big[\mu^{\infty}_{s}(y)-\mu^{\infty}_{s}*D^{N}(y)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.34)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\nabla p_{t-s}(x-y)\mathrm{D}_{p}H(y,\nabla u^{N}_{s}(y))\Big[\mu^{\infty}_{s}*D^{N}(y)-\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N}(y)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s.

We can treat (
4.33
)
by means of Lemma

A.6
and then handle (
4.34
)
by means of Lemma

A.3

and
(
4.25
). Regarding (
4.32
),
we can bound it by using (
4.27
).
∎

Theorem
4.8
is implicitly understood for
\mu_{0}^{N}=m
but, as in the case of Theorem
4.2
, it remains valid even if (

⁢
F

B
N

) starts from the truncated measure
\mu^{N}_{0}=m*D^{N}
instead of
m
. To prove this, it is enough to use Proposition
4.4
in place of Theorem
4.2
, and to notice that
\lVert m-m*D^{N}\rVert_{2}
and
\lVert m-m*D^{N}\rVert_{\infty}
can be easily controlled with the desired rates thanks to Lemmas
A.5
and
A.6
.

Remark 4.9
.

By combining Proposition
3.16
, Theorem
4.8
and Lemma
A.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}\rVert_{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}*D^{N}-\mu^{N}*D^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1},

while from Lemma
A.6
we get

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}*D^{N}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1-\frac{d}{2}}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}.

Remark 4.10
.

We believe that
the
L^{2}
estimate in Theorem
4.8
can be extended to the
H^{r}
norm, for

{\lfloor q-2-d/2\rfloor}>r>\frac{d}{2}
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
. More precisely, it should hold

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl(\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{t}-\mu^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2,r}+\lVert u^{\infty}_{t}-u^{N}_{t}\rVert_{2,r}\Bigr)\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1-r}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1},

for any
\lfloor q-2-d/2\rfloor>r>\frac{d}{2}
,
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
. This could be shown by following the techniques used to prove Propositions

3.7

and

3.14
.

4.3.
Convergence of the value functions

In this section we investigate the convergence of the value functions, as a byproduct of the convergence of the optimal control proved in Subsections
4.1
and
4.2
. Let us recall the shape of the value functions, when the costs are evaluated at the optimal controls:

(4.35)

\displaystyle V^{\infty}(t,m)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T})+\int_{t}^{T}\left\{\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{s})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}L(x,\alpha_{s}^{*,\infty}(x))\mu^{\infty}_{s}(\!\,\mathrm{d}x)\right\}\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle V^{N}(t,m)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N})+\int_{t}^{T}\left\{\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}L(x,\alpha_{s}^{*,N}(x))(\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N})(\!\,\mathrm{d}x)\right\}\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
(\mu^{\infty},u^{\infty})
and
(\mu^{N},u^{N})
are the solutions to the forward-backward systems (

⁢
F

B
∞

) and (

⁢
F

B
N

) respectively, and
\alpha^{*,\infty}(\cdot)=-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty}(\cdot))
and
\alpha^{*,N}(\cdot)=-\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{N}(\cdot))
(see Proposition
3.25
).
Notice that, for any
m
satisfying Assumption
2.10
and
N
large enough, it holds that
V^{N}(t,m)=V^{N}(t,m*D^{N})
. This trivially follows from the definition of
V^{N}
, which involves only
\mu^{N}*D^{N}
. Therefore, it is equivalent to consider (

⁢
F

B
N

) starting from
m
or the truncated measure
m*D^{N}
.

Before stating the result of this section, let us introduce a family of subspaces of
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
. For any
R>0
, we set

(4.36)

\mathcal{B}^{q,\gamma}_{R}:=\{m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\text{ s.t. Assumption \ref{hp: 3} holds and }\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}\leq R\}.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let Assumption
2.2
hold. Then, there exists a positive constant
C=C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,\gamma,q,R)
such that

\sup_{m\in\mathcal{B}^{q,\gamma}_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T)}\lvert V(t,m)-V^{N}(t,m)\rvert\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,\gamma,q,R)}{N^{q-1}}.

Proof.

From (
4.35
) we have, for any
t\in[0,T]
and
m\in\mathcal{B}^{q,\gamma}_{R}
,

\displaystyle\lvert V(t,m)-V^{N}

\displaystyle(t,m)\rvert

(4.37)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\mathcal{G}(\mu^{\infty}_{T})-\mathcal{G}(\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N})\right|

(4.38)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lvert\int_{t}^{T}\left\{\mathcal{F}(\mu^{\infty}_{s})-\mathcal{F}(\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N})\right\}\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert

(4.39)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lvert\int_{t}^{T}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}L(x,\alpha_{s}^{*,\infty}(x))\mu^{\infty}_{s}(\!\,\mathrm{d}x)-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}L(x,\alpha_{s}^{*,N}(x))(\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N})(\!\,\mathrm{d}x)\right\}\,\mathrm{d}s\right\rvert.

We notice from Assumption
2.2
that
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
are Lipschitz with respect to the
L^{2}
norm. Indeed, because their derivatives are bounded, both
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{F}
and
\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{G}
are Lipschitz continuous with respect to the
1
-Wasserstein distance and thus also with respect to
L^{2}
(see Remark
A.4
).
Then, we have

\eqref{eqn: conv_value_1}\leq C(\mathcal{G})\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{T}-\mu^{N}_{T}*D^{N}\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1},

where the last inequality follows directly from Remark
4.9
. Similarly, we get

\eqref{eqn: conv_value_2}\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}.

For the last term, we are going to exploit the boundedness of the optimal controls obtained in §
3.1.1
, together with the convergence results obtained in
Subsections
4.1
and
4.2
. It holds

(
4.39
)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left(L(x,\alpha_{s}^{*,\infty}(x))-L(x,\alpha_{s}^{*,N}(x))\right)\mu^{\infty}_{s}(\!\,\mathrm{d}x)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg\rvert

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg\lvert\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}L(x,\alpha_{s}^{*,N}(x))\left(\mu^{\infty}_{s}-\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N}\right)(\!\,\mathrm{d}x)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\left(\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\nabla u^{\infty}_{s}-\nabla u^{N}_{s}\rVert_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q)\int_{0}^{T}\lVert\mu^{\infty}_{s}-\mu^{N}_{s}*D^{N}\rVert_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C(d,T,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G},H,q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\gamma})}{N^{q-1}}\lVert m\rVert^{2}_{2,q-1}.

In the inequalities above, we first used the fact that
L(\cdot,\alpha^{*,\infty})=\mathrm{D}_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty})\cdot\nabla u^{\infty}-H(\cdot,\nabla u^{\infty})
(and the analogue formula with
\alpha^{*,N}
and
\nabla u^{N}
) in order to control the difference of the two Lagrangians. Then, we can use the estimates on
H
and
\mathrm{D}_{p}H
following from Assumption
2.2
- (H.5) together with Remark
3.12
. Finally, to deal with the first summand, we used Cauchy-Schwarz inequality together with Theorem
4.2
and the boundedness of
\mu^{\infty}
, whilst for the second one we used Remark
4.9
.
∎

Appendix A

We present here a collection of classical results on Fourier series and technical remarks that we have used all along our discussion. Let us recall that
i^{2}=-1
and
\lvert k\rvert=\max_{j=1,\dots,d}\lvert k_{j}\rvert
, for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. First, let us state a well known fact about Fourier coefficients (see, for instance,
[
19
, Theorem 3.3.9]
).

Proposition A.1
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}
. For a function
\varphi\colon\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, assume that, for all multi-indices
\lvert a\rvert\leq q
,
\partial^{a}\varphi
exists and is integrable . Then

\lvert\widehat{\varphi}(k)\rvert\leq\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{2\pi}\right)^{q}\frac{\max_{\lvert a\rvert=q}\lvert\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-8.34418pt\hbox{$\partial^{a}\varphi$}\hskip-8.34418pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-441.65944pt\hskip 441.65944pt}\rvert}{\lvert k\rvert^{q}},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},k\neq 0.

Remark A.2
.

From Proposition
A.1
, it follows that if
\varphi\in H^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,

\lvert\widehat{\varphi}(k)\rvert\leq\frac{C(d,q)}{\lvert k\rvert^{q}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{2,q},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},k\neq 0.

We present now a series of results concerning the relation between
L^{\infty}
and
H^{q}
norms for truncated Fourier series (or for the remainders). They play a key role in almost all our results, especially the ones in Section
4
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let us consider a function
\mu
in
H^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
q\geq 0
. Then, for a positive constant
C=C(d)
it holds that

\lVert\mu*D^{N}\rVert_{2,q}\leq\lVert\mu\rVert_{2,q},\quad\lVert\mu*D^{N}\rVert_{\infty}\leq C(d)N^{\frac{d}{2}}\lVert\mu\rVert_{2}.

Moreover, if
q>\frac{d}{2}
, there exists a positive constant
C=C(d,q)
such that

\lVert\mu*D^{N}\rVert_{\infty}\leq C(d,q)\lVert\mu\rVert_{2,q}.

Proof.

By definition of Sobolev norm and
D^{N}
, it follows

\lVert\mu*D^{N}\rVert^{2}_{2,q}=\sum_{\lvert k\rvert\leq N}(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{q}\lvert\hat{\mu}(k)\rvert^{2}\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{q}\lvert\hat{\mu}(k)\rvert^{2}=\lVert\mu\rVert^{2}_{2,q}.

Moreover, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Parseval’s theorem

\lvert\mu*D^{N}(x)\rvert\leq\sum_{\lvert k\rvert\leq N}\lvert\hat{\mu}(k)\rvert\leq C(d)N^{\frac{d}{2}}\left(\sum_{\lvert k\rvert\leq N}\lvert\hat{\mu}(k)\rvert^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}C(d)N^{\frac{d}{2}}\lVert\mu\rVert_{2}.

Finally, if
q>\frac{d}{2}
, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality it follows

\lvert\mu*D^{N}(x)\rvert\leq\sum_{\lvert k\rvert\leq N}\lvert\hat{\mu}(k)\rvert(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{\frac{q}{2}}(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{-\frac{q}{2}}\leq\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\frac{1}{(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\!\!\lVert\mu\rVert_{2,q}=C(d,q)\lVert\mu\rVert_{2,q}.

∎

Remark A.4
.

If
\mu,\nu
have densities in
L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, it holds

\lVert\mu-\nu\rVert_{\rm TV}=\sup_{\lVert h\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1}\left\lvert\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}h(x)(\mu(x)-\nu(x))\,\mathrm{d}x\right\rvert\leq C(d)\lVert\mu-\nu\rVert_{2},

since
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
\lVert\mu-\nu\rVert_{2}=\sup_{h\in L^{2}}\lvert\langle\mu-\nu,h\rangle|
. Similarly, if
\mu,\nu\in H^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, the inclusion

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\subset H^{-q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
entails
\lVert\mu-\nu\rVert_{\rm TV}\leq C(d)\lVert\mu-\nu\rVert_{2,q}
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let us consider a function
\mu
in
H^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
q\geq 0
. Then, it holds that

\lVert\mu-\mu*D^{N}\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\lVert\mu\rVert_{2,q}}{N^{q}}.

Proof.

The case
q=0
is immediate. For
q>0
, it holds

\displaystyle\lVert\mu-\mu*D^{N}\rVert^{2}_{2}=\sum_{\lvert k\rvert>N}\lvert\widehat{\mu}(k)\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lvert k\rvert>N}\lvert\widehat{\mu}(k)\rvert^{2}(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{q}(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{-q}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N^{2q}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\lvert\widehat{\mu}(k)\rvert^{2}(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{q}=\frac{\lVert\mu\rVert_{2,q}^{2}}{N^{2q}}.

∎

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\varphi\in H^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
for a certain
q>\frac{d}{2}
. Then, there exists a positive constant
C=C(d,q)
such that

\lVert\varphi-\varphi*D^{N}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{C(d,q)}{N^{q-\frac{d}{2}}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{2,q}.

Proof.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi-\varphi*D^{N}\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\lvert k\rvert>N}\lvert\widehat{\varphi}(k)\rvert=\sum_{\lvert k\rvert>N}\lvert\widehat{\varphi}(k)\rvert(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{\frac{q}{2}}(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{-\frac{q}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\sum_{\lvert k\rvert>N}\frac{1}{(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{q}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{\lvert k\rvert>N}(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{q}\lvert\widehat{\varphi}(k)\rvert^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C(d,q)}{N^{q-\frac{d}{2}}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{2,q},

where we used that fact that
\sum_{\lvert k\rvert>N}\frac{1}{(1+\lvert k\rvert^{2})^{q}}\leq\frac{C(d,q)}{N^{2q-d}}
since
q>\frac{d}{2}
.
∎

Remark A.7
.

From Lemma
A.6
we can deduce some estimates for the truncated Fourier series. Indeed, if
\varphi\in H^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, with
q>\frac{d}{2}
, then

(A.1)

\lVert\varphi*D^{N}\rVert_{\infty}\leq\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\infty}+\frac{C(d,q)}{N^{q-\frac{d}{2}}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{2,q}.

Of course, if
\varphi
belongs also to
\mathrm{C}^{q}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we can exchange the
H^{q}
norm with the
\mathrm{C}^{q}
norm.

For our discussion in Subsection
3.1.1
, we also need some standard estimates for the heat kernel on the torus, that we collect here for simplicity.

Lemma A.8
.

For
t\in[0,T]
, let us denote by
p=p_{t}(x)
, the usual heat kernel over
\mathbb{T}^{d}
.
Then, for every
t\in[0,T]
it holds

\lVert p_{t}\rVert_{2}\leq C(d,T)t^{-\frac{d}{4}},\quad\lVert\nabla p_{t}\rVert_{2}\leq C(d,T)t^{-(\frac{d}{4}+\frac{1}{2})}.
